The moment money moves from your bank into a personal UPI handle, into a crypto wallet, or into an account that is not the registered name of a company you have verified, the regulator cannot help you, and the money is gone. Regulated companies take money only into their own corporate account.

Four. There is no trace. Search for the company on the Ministry of Corporate Affairs database, which is public, and look for the name, the registered office, and the year of incorporation. If nothing comes back, or if the address is a co working box, or if the phone number goes to a mobile that is not in the company's own directory, assume the entity is a shell.

Five. The family is the witness and the bait. This one is very Indian, and it is the last. Many scams that seem to end at your account end at your cousin's account, because a cousin who lost money will call you, and then two more, and then the family group chat. A scam that reaches your family group chat is already halfway to your parents. When a relative is under pressure, do not follow their proof screenshots. Ask for the company's name, and run it through the checks above.

### What to do in the first hour, and after

If you have just lost money, there is a narrow window where it can sometimes be stopped. This is not a promise, it is an average. The Reserve Bank of India has put fraud handling rules on banks, and for card and UPI fraud, banks are expected to investigate and, in eligible cases, to compensate. The practical sequence is this. First, call your bank's fraud line and freeze the account. Second, if it was a card transaction you did not make, ask for a charge back. For card fraud where an unauthorised card was used, the bank carries liability under the Reserve Bank of India rules, and reporting within 60 minutes of the transaction, or within 2 hours for some card issuers, is the window that keeps the liability firmly on the bank's side. Third, report on the National Cyber Crime Portal at cybercrime.gov.in. This portal is the single national window. You file a complaint there, it is routed to the cyber cell of the police, and the portal has a 24 hour phone line, 1930, staffed day and night. Fourth, within the same day, file a formal written complaint with the local police station. The portal filing and the police complaint reinforce each other, and a portal complaint without a police case is a half measure.

There is also a specific mechanism for UPI. The National Payments Corporation of India, which runs UPI, has a fraud reversal process. Your UPI operator, which is your bank, can send a reversal request to the receiving operator, and that request can only be accepted if the receiving account has not withdrawn the money, or if the account is still open and can be frozen. This is why the clock matters. UPI reversals work best when filed within hours, and they stop working once the money is split, moved to crypto, or pulled out of linked accounts. So if you can, call your UPI operator as soon as you know the money has gone out.

One more point. Banks are required to report fraud to the Reserve Bank of India and to credit bureaus under a fraud reporting framework. This means that even where you cannot recover the money, filing the report creates a record that can stop the same person from defrauding others, and it is also what a lawyer will need if the matter reaches the courts. Even a small complaint is worth the ten minutes.

### Card skimming and phishing, the two local patterns

Card skimming in India has a very specific face. The device is a small reader stuck on to an ATM keypad, or on to a fuel station card slot, or on a bus payment machine. It reads the magnetic stripe of your card, and a tiny camera, often hidden in a loose phone case, records your pin as you type it. The attacker then makes a clone of your card, usually with a printer a few cities away, and cashes it out. You may not even notice the machine, but you will notice that your card was blocked, or that a cash back transaction appeared at a fuel station. The protection is not to write your pin on the card, and it is to watch for anything that looks added on to the keypad, and to report any machine that looks altered to the bank. If you see a loose or sticky card reader, do not use that machine, and tell the bank where it is.

Phishing in India has its own shapes. The most common is a one time password that arrives in your messages when you have not requested it. That means somebody is trying to authorise a transaction in your account, and you must call your bank, not the phone number in the message. The next is a link in a message that looks like your bank, but the address is a long string of numbers, and it asks for your net banking login. The next is a WhatsApp forward that says your bank sent a link to verify your account, moving fast because a real friend is forwarding it in good faith. The last is a UPI request from a name you do not recognise. The general rule: your bank will never send a login link to your WhatsApp, and a UPI request from an unknown name is not a gift, it is a bill, and a very expensive one if you pay it.

### Loan app harassment, and the legal protection

A separate and very painful problem is the loan app. Small unregulated apps, not banks, not the National Housing Bank, not the main non banking finance companies, promise a loan with no documents and a few taps. The app, once you install it, asks for permission to access your contacts, your location, your microphone, and your SMS folder. The last one is the problem. If you default on a small loan of ten thousand rupees, the app calls your mother, your in laws, your office, and your neighbours, in a loop, with abusive language, threats, and messages to every number in your phone. This is a known and documented form of harassment, and it has driven real tragedies.

The law has moved on this. The Reserve Bank of India has issued circulars, going back to 2016, saying that only registered entities can lend, and that loan collection must follow a prescribed fair manner. The 2023 and 2024 directions say that a registered lender cannot use abusive language, cannot contact your relatives or employer about your personal loan, and cannot use the contacts in your phone for collection, because the app must not have access to them at all. The Digital Personal Data Protection Act of 2023 gives you a right to object to the collection of your personal data. If you are being harassed by a loan app, you can file a complaint on the Reserve Bank of India's customer grievance portal, and you can also file a police complaint. The legal protection exists. What does not exist is a fast remedy, so the practical advice is: if you can pay the loan, pay it; if you cannot, tell the app in writing, let them harass the phone and not the family, organise your finances, and close the default. And never, ever, take a new loan app to repay an old one. That is how a ten thousand problem becomes a ten lakh problem.

### The fake Income Tax department call

A variation that is very common in India is the call that pretends to be from the Income Tax Department. They say your account has been flagged, that a notice is coming, and you must be careful. They quote your own Permanent Account Number, your own mobile, sometimes your own bank, to look official. Then they say that to prevent the notice, you must move money to a safe account, or pay a penalty to a UPI number, or share your one time password, or your Aadhaar number and a photo of your bank passbook, or visit a government office and bring cash. None of these are real. The Income Tax Department does not ask for money on a UPI handle, does not ask for your one time password, and will not deliver a notice by a phone call that pressures you into acting within the hour. If you receive such a call, calmly ask for the officer's name, the office, and a reference number, end the call, and visit the official Income Tax portal to see whether there is a notice on your account. In most cases there will not be.

### The family inheritance scam

This one is old, and it keeps coming back. A stranger, by email, by a lawyer's letter, or by a WhatsApp message, tells you that you are the heir of a distant relative, that a cousin, or an uncle, or an uncle's estate in a foreign country, is about to be divided, and that you will get the largest share. The request is for an initial payment: a duty, a lawyer's cost, a customs fee, a transfer fee, ranging from a few thousand to a few lakhs of rupees, a percentage of a fortune you have never seen. This is one of the oldest scams in the fraud world. If you have received such a message, do not reply, do not send the first payment, and do not hand over your bank details. A relative you have never met, who is about to leave you a large fortune and needs you to pay first, is a person who has no will and no money. The check is easy. If the relative is real, they will have a will, they will have a lawyer, and they will not ask for the first rupee from you.

### The Non Resident Indian scam

The last one in this set is specific to Indians living abroad, and to the money they send home. The scam targets the Non Resident Indian, a person outside India for work or settlement, with a promise to open a Non Resident Ordinary account, to arrange a Non Resident Investor Repatriable account, or to obtain an NRI loan on the strength of an overseas salary. The request is for a fee, paid to a personal account, or for the funds to be moved into an account that is not in your name, but in the name of a middleman who will forward it. The risk is two fold. On one hand, the money is lost to the middleman. On the other, the moment your money enters the country through a route that is not a bank licensed to take NRI funds, you are inside the foreign exchange rules, and the Reserve Bank of India and the Enforcement Directorate can treat it as a violation of the Foreign Exchange Management and Development Act, because the money came in through an unregistered route. The protection is that all NRI funds, whether for an account or for a loan, move only through a bank authorised to deal in that money. Never pay a personal account, and never let a relative abroad take the remittance and forward it, because that is exactly what a money mule does.

A final note on this chapter. If you are reading this after a loss, not before, the steps still work. Freeze the account, file on the National Cyber Crime Portal, call 1930, file a written police complaint, and report to the Reserve Bank of India. No one will recover your money as fast as you would like, but the record keeps the money from other people, and it is what a lawyer will need if you ever reach a court. The scammers count on shame. Most victims do not speak, so the records that banks and police actually receive are a small fraction of the real damage, and that is why the next victim keeps being you, or your family. Breaking the silence is the first financial decision of this chapter, and it costs nothing.

## Chapter 33: The Grey Zone. Legal Strategies That Are Not Illegal, and the Ones That Are

This chapter lives in the space between the honest bank and the outright crime. It is the space of the family structure, of the non resident status, of the gift, of the trust, of the partition, of the disclosure. A large share of Indian households, and a very large share of the tax department's attention, sits in this zone. The difference between a smart plan and a bad plan here is not whether the money moved. It is whether the move was told, told completely, told before it was needed, and whether the structure itself, not just the transfer, was real.

### The Hindu Undivided Family, and how far it can go

Let us start with the Hindu Undivided Family, usually shortened to HUF. An HUF is a family unit under Hindu personal law that can hold property in its own name. It is a separate entity from you and from your spouse. It has its own Permanent Account Number, its own bank account, and its own statement of account. It is taxed at the same slabs as a person. The legal limit of members is seven. The head of the family, called the Karta, is the one who speaks for it and signs for it.

The tax benefit of an HUF is not a special rate. It is a second box, or a third box, that holds income at the same slabs. If you are in the highest slab on your own, and your spouse is in the highest slab on her own, an HUF that earns income of its own is a third box that starts at zero, and that is where the extra room comes from. The HUF can hold property, it can hold investments, it can earn rent, and the income that the HUF earns belongs to the HUF, and that is what is slabs.

The limits are important, and they are often misunderstood. An HUF does not automatically absorb your salary. If you earn 30 lakh rupees, you cannot move that 30 lakh into the HUF because you are a member. The salary is yours, because it was earned by you, not by the family. An HUF earns income when it has assets of its own, or when it lends money at a real interest, or when it validly receives a gift made to the HUF as a family, or when the Karta acts on behalf of the family to buy a plot and to rent it out. If the HUF has no asset and no income of its own, it is just a registration number, and it does nothing for you. If it has assets, brought in through a real and honest route, then the income is a real income of a real family, and the slabs are a real saving.

The trap is in the transfer. A gift from you to your HUF, where you are both the Karta and the maker of the gift, is a gift from yourself to yourself, and the department will look at it that way. A loan from you to the HUF at a very low interest is not a gift, it is a loan, and it is priced, and a price that is too low is a red flag. The honest way to build the HUF is to give it its own asset: buy a plot in the HUF name at the time of purchase, with funds coming from your declared income, declare the HUF as the owner from day one, and let the plot earn rent into the HUF account. That is a clean move. The clean version works. The messy version, where money is cycled through a loan, or a gift, or a relative, is the version that gets called out.

### The family trust, when it makes sense, and when it is just paperwork

The second structure is the family trust. A family trust is a deed, a legal document, where one or more people, called the trustees, agree to hold and manage assets for the benefit of named people, called the beneficiaries. The person who makes the trust is the settlor, or the author. The trust is a separate legal entity. It has a Permanent Account Number. It holds the property, it manages it, and it distributes income to the beneficiaries, or holds it for them. The beneficiaries are usually your children, or your parents, or a class defined in the deed.

A family trust makes sense in three situations. First, where you have a lot of property that is not all in one name, and you want it managed by one entity, in one account, under one set of rules. Second, where you are a businessperson, a doctor, or a lawyer, who is exposed to professional claims, and you want a layer between your personal assets and your professional risk. Third, where you have an NRI child, or a child living in a different country, and you want the assets held by a structure that is not tied to any one country's tax residency. In all three, the trust is a real administrative tool.

A family trust does not make sense when the only reason is a lower tax rate. The income of the trust is distributed to the beneficiaries and taxed in their hands at their slabs, which is the same as if they held it themselves. The trust does not give you a lower rate on capital gains, and it does not let you escape the slabs. What it does is give you a separate body, a separate set of documents, and a way to manage assets for people who are not in the room. It is a structure, not a tax cut. If you set up a trust where the maker, the manager, and the sole beneficiary are all the same person, and that person takes the money out every year, then the trust is a coat you wear, and it will not survive the examination, because a trust where the maker, the manager, and the beneficiary are the same person is not a trust in the way the law means it. The honest family trust has other real people in it, and a purpose that is not just your tax.

### The residence question, and the 182 day rule

A large part of the grey zone, for Indian families that cross a border, is the question of residence. For tax, you are a resident of India if you are in India for 182 days or more in a financial year, or if you meet the combined tests of 120 days and 12 years out of the last 8 that the Income Tax Act uses. The short version: if you live abroad for your whole financial year, and are in India for fewer than 182 days, you are a non resident, and you pay Indian tax only on income that is from India, that is, income earned in India, received in India, or accrued in India. Your overseas salary, overseas interest, and overseas capital gains are not taxed in India while you are a non resident.

This is the part people get wrong on both sides. Some people who left in 2019 and have been abroad ever since still file as residents, because they have not updated their address and are still on their old plan, and they are paying Indian tax on overseas income. Others who left just after the end of the financial year, and spent most of the year in India, must be residents, and must report global income. The rule is not when you left, it is the count of days. Before you take a decision, count the days in the financial year, on both sides of the border, and if you are near the 182 day line, get the count in writing, because a few days of a family visit can flip you.

There is also the matter of money coming home. While you are a non resident, money you send home into a Non Resident Ordinary account is not taxable income, because it is not income, it is your own money, moved. The moment that money is moved into a resident's savings account, it is not taxable in the resident's hands, but it is still a transfer, and the department can ask for the source of the funds and proof of the move. So the route for the money should always be a bank transfer or a proper remittance through a bank, never through a relative's personal account, because a relative's account is a grey zone in the relative's name, not in yours.

### The Foreign Currency Non Resident account, and its benefit

A specific account for the Indian expatriate is the Foreign Currency Non Resident account, usually shortened to FCNR. It holds the foreign currency you brought in, dollars, euros, or pounds, in a currency account that is not converted to rupees. There are two main forms: the FCNR account, which holds the foreign currency itself, and the Non Resident Ordinary account, which takes the money you earn abroad and is usually converted to rupees. The benefits of the FCNR account are that the money is not taxed in India while it sits in the currency, the interest it earns in the foreign currency is not taxed in India, and the money can be repatriated, that is, moved back abroad, freely, without a cap. For someone who is abroad and wants to hold savings in a currency, it is a clean holding place. The rule people trip over is that it is for non residents only. The moment you become a resident, the account must be closed or converted, and the money is subject to resident rules.

The decision of when to close it is a decision about your days. If you are sure you will stay abroad for the financial year, keep it. If you have come home for good, close it and move the money, because a non resident account in the name of a resident is a red flag, and also a violation of the foreign exchange rules.

### Non Resident tax, resident tax, and the remittance question

This brings us to the real question families sit with: am I paying tax where I should, and on what? A resident of India is taxed on global income, income earned anywhere in the world. A non resident is taxed on income that is from India, or received in India, and not on income earned abroad that stays abroad. The word remittance comes in when a non resident brings foreign earned money home, into a resident account or a resident's account, because that is a receipt in India, and it is treated as income in India. This is the classic NRI trap: a non resident who earns abroad and sends the money home to a family savings account is creating a taxable event in India, not a transfer event. The clean path for an NRI who has come home with savings is to keep the savings in a Foreign Currency Non Resident account, or in a non repatriable account, to convert slowly, to file the tax that is due, and to keep the paper of every conversion, so that the money in India is explained, not just present.

### Section 132, the search, and what the officer actually looks for

Let us move to the section every Indian family hears in whispers: section 132 of the Income Tax Act, the power of an officer to enter a place, to search it, to seize money and records and computers. A search is not a punishment. It is a tool, used when the department believes income has not been declared, property has not been explained, or required records are missing. The officer comes with a warrant and a notice that names you, or the place, and says what is being looked for. In most cases you are not an enemy of the state, and you are not under arrest. What is at stake is the explanation of what is there.

What does the officer look for, in the practical order? One, cash, large amounts that do not match a declared source. Two, bank passbooks and statements showing money coming in that is not explained. Three, property documents, that is, sale deeds, in your name or a relative's name, that you cannot explain. Four, investments, a demat account or a mutual fund statement, with a balance that does not match your return. Five, the computer, which is the modern drawer, where the email, the chat, and the photo of the receipt live. The practical lesson is that you should know, before any notice arrives, what is in the house that can be seen, and be able to explain each item in one sentence. A house that cannot explain its assets is a house at risk of a search that turns into a case.

### The Benami property law, and when it actually applies

Next is the Benami Property law. The word benami means without a name, and the law says that you cannot hold property in another person's name, or let another person hold property in yours, when the real owner is you, unless the law accepts a reason for the mismatch. The Benami Transactions Prohibition Act of 1988, strengthened by an amendment in 2016, makes such holding a criminal offence, not just a tax mismatch, with confiscation of the property, a fine up to 50 per cent of the property's value, and imprisonment. The reasons the law accepts are specific: a relative who is a minor, a spouse who is not a relative by law, a friend who is genuinely the owner, or a person who cannot hold property in their own name, like a public servant barred from doing so. In every other case, the law says the real owner is the person who paid, and the paper is a lie.

The trap many families fall into is holding a property in a brother's name because the brother is unmarried and, in the family's thinking, not on the tax list. If, a few years later, the property is sold and the sale money goes to you, and not to the brother, the department asks who really owns it, and the brother says it is his and you say it is ours, and the Benami law exists exactly for this. It will be resolved in your disfavor, because in the law's eyes the one who paid is the one who owns. The honest version is that if the property is in your brother's name, the brother is the owner, the money is his, you do not use it, you do not claim it, and you do not live in it without his written permission, which is a document.

### The gift tax, and when a gift becomes a tax event

The next grey zone is the gift. Under the Income Tax Act, a gift received from a relative is not a taxable event, provided it is a true gift, without any payment in return. A relative, in the gift sense, is a spouse, and lineal relatives: your father, your mother, your son, your daughter, your grandfather, your grandmother, and siblings. The moment a gift comes from outside that list, it is a taxable event, and it is taxed in your hands at your slab, because a gift from a stranger is not a gift in the tax sense, it is income.

The second half is where you are the giver. If you give an asset to your spouse, the income that asset earns, the rent, the interest, the dividend, can be clubbed back to you under the clubbing rules, because the asset is yours, moved to her. This is a real and working rule, and it is why some families do not move income assets to the spouse, and keep them in the HUF, where the income belongs to the HUF. The honest gift within the family is the one that is recorded: write it down, keep it in a file, and report it in your return. A gift that is not reported is not a gift, it is a shadow, and a shadow is exactly what the search is looking for.

### Section 139, the cost of not filing, and the penalty question

Next is the matter of not filing your return. Section 139 of the Income Tax Act lays down who must file. Sub section 4 deals with the case where you were required to file and did not. If you have income above the basic exemption limit and you skip the filing, the consequences pile up: a late fee under section 234F, interest under section 234A, and the right of the department to estimate your income on its own, which is almost always higher than what you would have reported. In serious cases, wilful failure to file invites prosecution under the sections that carry up to two years of imprisonment. The 20 per cent figure that people quote in conversation about a non filing penalty is a loose number; the real penalties are the late fee, the interest, the estimation, and in the case of misreporting income under section 270A, a penalty that can reach up to 200 per cent of the tax you should have paid. The practical advice is that you do not need to memorise the 20 per cent. You need to know that not filing, in a year where you must file, is not a neutral act. It creates a liability, it makes the search easier, because a person who does not file is a person who is not in the department's data, and that is exactly the profile the search looks for.

### The money laundering law, and what tainted money means in practice

The last piece of the grey zone is the money laundering law, the Prevention of Money Laundering Act, usually shortened to PMLA. The law says that you cannot take money that is the proceeds of a crime, and move it, hold it, or use it in a way that makes it look clean. The word tainted means that the money is the proceeds of a crime, and the crime is one of a list of scheduled offences. The list includes cheating, criminal breach of trust, corruption under the criminal law, the foreign exchange offences, and the drug offences. The point of the law is that you cannot take the money of a crime, put it into a property, a bank, or a business, and make it look like the money of a clean person.

In practice, for a family, the question is not whether you are a money launderer. It is whether your money can be explained, from end to end, without a gap. If you buy a plot, and the payment comes from a relative's account, and the relative cannot explain why they paid, the plot is not clean, and the relative is not clean, and both of you are in the grey zone, and both of you are at risk of a case. If the money came from your declared salary, was in your account, and you paid from your account, and the paper is in your name, the plot is clean, and you are clean, and the department cannot find a gap, because there is no gap. The test of tainted money is the test of the gap. If you can explain every rupee of a big move, in a paper trail, you are not laundering. You are just a person who is careful.

One final note on this chapter. The grey zone is not a place you live in. It is a place you pass through, quickly, with paper, and with a declaration. The difference between a smart plan and a bad plan is not the structure. It is how early, and how completely, the structure is declared. An HUF that is declared, a trust that is declared, a gift that is declared, a non resident status that is declared, a search that is met with a file: all of these are the same thing, which is a family that tells the truth early and completely, and a family that is not caught in the middle, because the middle is where the search is, and the middle is where the heavy penalty is, and the middle is where the case is.

## Chapter 34: The Illegal Zone. What Is Actually Against the Law in Indian Personal Finance

The previous chapter was the grey zone: a place you pass through, quickly, with paper. This chapter is the place you do not enter. The things here are not just risky, they are against the law, and the against the law has a name, a section, and a penalty, and in some cases a minimum sentence, not an average one. We will go through the list, say the name of the law, and say the name of the penalty, so that when you hear these words in a family conversation, in a police station, or in a court, you know what is being said.

### Tax evasion, not tax avoidance

The first distinction, and the one every Indian should know in their bones, is the difference between tax evasion and tax avoidance. Tax avoidance is using the law, legally, to pay less tax: structuring your income, your assets, your family, in a way the law gives you a lower number. Tax avoidance is not illegal, and it is not even frowned on; it is what Chapter 33 was about. Tax evasion is not paying a tax the law says you must pay, by hiding the income, by making a false return, by keeping two sets of books, by taking cash that is not reported, by selling a property at a lower price to the department than the real price, or by giving a gift and declaring it at a figure that is not the real one. Tax evasion is a criminal offence, not a financial mistake. The penalty is not a fee. It is a percentage of the tax, a prosecution, and in wilful cases, a sentence.

The laws that apply are the Income Tax Act, where section 270A is the penalty section for underreporting and misreporting, and the criminal law, which is now the Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, the code that replaced the Indian Penal Code in 2024, where cheating and forgery carry their own sentences. The distinction to hold in your head: avoidance is a structure, evasion is a lie. A structure can be defended in front of a lawyer. A lie cannot be defended by anyone, because the lie is the evidence, and you cannot argue a lie in a court. You can only answer to it.

### Black money, and the Benami criminal offence

The next illegal zone is the zone of black money: money that is not declared, not in the paper trail, held in cash, in a relative's name, in a property that is not in your name, or in a business that is not yours. The law that specifically targets holding property in a false name is the Benami Transactions Prohibition Act. Since the 2016 amendment, the holding is not just a tax mismatch, it is a criminal offence. The penalty is confiscation of the property, a fine up to 50 per cent of the property's value, and imprisonment of up to seven years. The word confiscated matters. It means the property is taken, and in many cases it is not returned even if the case is later settled.

The practical rule that keeps you out of this zone is the paper trail. If a property is in your name, you paid it from your account, and the money came from your declared income, you are not in the Benami zone. If a property is in your brother's name, and you paid for it, and you asked your brother to say it is his, you are in the Benami zone, and you have been in it since the day you paid. The fact that the brother has not complained does not take you out, because the law looks at the payer, not at the paper.

### Money laundering under the PMLA: ten years, and the seizure

The next zone is the actual money laundering under the Prevention of Money Laundering Act. The offence of moving, holding, or using the proceeds of a scheduled crime carries a sentence of up to ten years, and a fine that is the value of the property involved, or a few crores of rupees, whichever is higher. In a big case the fine is the property itself, which is the point: you cannot afford to keep the asset the crime bought you, because the penalty is the asset.

The second part of the law is attachment. The Enforcement Directorate can seize your property, your bank accounts, and your business in the middle of a case, not at the end, not after a verdict, but in the middle, so that you cannot sell it, move it, or use it while the case runs. Attachment is not a punishment. It is a precaution, and it stays until the court orders otherwise, and the court orders otherwise slowly. The practical effect of attachment is that you are in a case, with no money, with no property, in the middle, and the middle is where the family breaks, where the business dies, and where the person who moved the money becomes, overnight, a person who has nothing.

The lesson of this zone is not that you should not be a money launderer. It is that this law is not a place you argue your way out of. It is a place you do not enter, because once you enter, you do not leave the way you came in. You leave with a fine that equals what you did, a sentence of up to ten years, an attachment that is not a verdict, and a record that is not a memory. And you leave it to the family to pay, because the family is the thing you did it for, and the family is the thing that pays for it.

### Criminal breach of trust, and the cheating sections

The next zone is the offence against a person, not against the department. The person hurt is the one who gave you the money, and the money is not back. The criminal law, which is now the Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita and was the Indian Penal Code, has two big families of sections here. Cheating, the old sections 415 to 420 of the Penal Code and their new equivalents, covers a person who, with the intention of causing damage, delivers something that is not what was promised. The punishment is imprisonment up to five to seven years, plus a fine, depending on the exact section. Criminal breach of trust, the old section 405 to 409, covers a person who, being in charge of property or money given to them in trust, moves it dishonestly for their own benefit. For a general breach of trust, the sentence is up to three years. For breach of trust by a servant of the person whose money it was, up to five to seven years. For a public servant, up to ten years.

This zone matters to families, because a lot of family money that goes wrong goes wrong here, not in the tax zone. The brother who was given the money to buy the plot, and the plot was not bought, and the money is gone, is a brother in the breach of trust zone, not the tax zone. The tax department will not get the money back. The police will, if the family files a case. And the family that does not file a case is the family that has told the brother, quietly, that his money was his to keep, and that quiet word is the most expensive thing in the family.

### Insider trading in Indian markets

The next zone is the market. The offence specific to the market is insider trading: using information that is not public to make a trade that is not fair. The law that applies is the framework of the Securities and Exchange Board of India, which is the regulator of the market, and specifically the insider trading regulations the Board has issued, which say that a person who trades on unpublished price sensitive information is committing an offence, and the penalty is a fine of up to three times the profit made, or a few crores of rupees, whichever is higher, and in serious cases, a prison sentence.

The detection is not a mystery and not a hunch. It is a pattern, and the regulator looks for the account that moved before the move: the account that bought the stock the week before a result was announced, and sold it the day after, and that is not a random person's account, but the account of a person near the information. A director, a manager, a consultant, or a person who was in the room where the decision was made. The regulator does not need to prove you knew the result. It needs to prove you were in the room, and that you moved. The move is the proof, the proof is the account, and the account is the paper that becomes the case. If you sit on a board, or advise a company, or work near the numbers, keep your family's money out of that company's stock until the news is public. One laptop, one family account, and one unpublicised result is all a case needs.

### Fictitious invoices, and the GST fraud

The next zone is the invoice. The Goods and Services Tax, usually shortened to GST, is the tax on the sale of goods and the provision of services, and a large part of how it is collected is through input tax credit: the tax you paid on what you bought is set off against the tax you collect on what you sell. The fraud is the fictitious invoice: a bill for a sale that never happened, used to claim a credit that was never earned. The law that applies is the GST Act itself, where making a false invoice, or claiming a credit on a false invoice, is an offence that carries the tax plus a penalty that can go up to the full amount of the fraud, plus interest, and in serious cases, a prosecution that can mean imprisonment.

The practical shape of this fraud in India is the shell company: a company with no product, no staff, no premises, no delivery, that exists only to issue invoices so that a real business can claim a credit it did not earn. The department's systems have become very good at matching invoices, and the pattern of a big buyer and a big seller with a ghost between them is exactly the pattern the systems are built to catch. The rule for a family business owner: every invoice in your books must be an invoice for a thing that actually moved, between parties that actually exist, with a delivery note or a record behind it. The moment the invoice becomes a device, you are not in the tax department's office, you are in the criminal court's, and the family business is the first thing that gets attached.

### Unregulated money lending, and the loan shark trap

The next zone is the loan that is not a loan. The law on lending in India is layered. The Reserve Bank of India regulates banks and the main non banking lenders, the Small Finance Banks, and the registered non banking finance companies. The states have their own money lending laws, and a money lender who lends without a state registration is operating in the illegal zone. The loan shark is the one at the end of that zone: a lender with no registration, no cap on interest, and no respect for the law at collection. The lending itself is the offence in many states, and the recovery, with violence, is an offence under the criminal law, on top of it.

The legal protection for the borrower has two parts. First, in most states, the interest a registered money lender can charge is capped, and an unregistered lender cannot legally recover the excess, and often cannot recover at all through the courts. Second, the 2023 and 2024 Reserve Bank of India directions, which we met in Chapter 32, say that collection must follow a fair manner, and the abusive, threatening, contact your relatives pattern is now a documented breach that can be reported to the Reserve Bank and to the police. The advice is simple and unglamorous: never take money from a lender you cannot name and register in two minutes. The loan that starts without a name is the one that ends with violence, and the one that ends with violence is the one that the family pays for twice, once in the interest and once in the fear.

### Forged documents, and the fake Permanent Account Number

The next zone is the document that is not a document. Forgery of a document that is used to obtain a benefit is a criminal offence under the criminal law, and the making or use of a fake Permanent Account Number is an offence under the Income Tax Act, which specifically punishes the use of a false Permanent Account Number, with a penalty and a prosecution. The specific shape in India is the fake Permanent Account Number made for a loan: a number that is not the person's own number, used to stand in for them in front of a lender. The moment that number is used, two people are in the criminal zone: the one who made it, and the one who used it. The protection is the original document, the number that is truly the person's own, verified on the Income Tax portal, because a verified number is the one that cannot be called false, and the one that cannot be called false is the one that survives the loan, and the loan is the one that survives the audit.

### What to do if you have already done it: the voluntary disclosure route

Now the most important part of this chapter, the part every family waiting in this zone is waiting for: the way out. If you have already moved money that is not clean, or made an invoice that was not real, or held a property in a false name, the way out is not the way in. The way out is to tell, before you are told.

There is a specific route for the Enforcement Directorate. A person who comes forward, discloses property that is the proceeds of a crime, and pays the tax and the penalty on it, gets treatment that is the penalty, not the prosecution. The one who discloses first, discloses completely, and discloses before the case is against them, gets the lighter side of the law. The one who waits for the attachment gets the heavier side. The application is made to the Enforcement Directorate, in writing, with the full picture: what the money is, where it came from, where it is now, and the tax that is due on it. The practical rule: the disclosure is only as good as its completeness, because a disclosure that hides half is not a disclosure, it is a smaller lie, and a smaller lie is the one the court calls a false one.

### Whistleblower protection, honestly

The last zone of this chapter is the one who tells: the person who did not do it, but saw it, and says it. India does not have a single strong Whistleblower Act covering the private sector. The Whistleblowers Protection Act of 2014 exists, but it is slow to come fully into force, and it is mostly aimed at public servants raising concerns about corruption. The real working protection for most people is in the Companies Act, section 177, which requires listed and large companies to have a mechanism for internal reporting of misconduct, and protects the person who reports in good faith from retaliation. There is also the provision in the Prevention of Money Laundering Act that rewards a person who gives information to the Enforcement Directorate that leads to a seizure.

The honest note, because this book does not sell comfort: the protection is not a promise. The law says the reporter in good faith is protected, and the practical reality is that the protection works only as well as the reporter's record does. A Whistleblower with paper, with dates, with names, with a copy of what they reported and when, is the one the protection reaches. A Whistleblower who only remembers is the one the family of the accused will break. If you are ever in the position of seeing something you cannot stay quiet about, the first thing you do is not tell anyone. The first thing you do is write it down, date it, keep it where the other side cannot touch it, and only then does it become something the law can hold.

## Chapter 35: Legal Documents Every Indian Should Have (But Probably Does Not)

We have looked at the scams, the grey zone, and the illegal zone. This chapter is where all of that meets paper. A large share of Indian families, even the careful ones, even the ones with a plan and a will, do not have the set of documents they need. And the set they do not have is the difference between a family that is in control and a family that is in the middle, and the middle is where the lawyer is, and the lawyer is where the cost is, and the cost is where the family pays. This chapter is the list, and the list is what you should have, what you should keep, and what you should update.

### The will: the checklist and the execution

An earlier chapter of this book covered what goes inside a will: who to name, what to leave, and how to divide it. This chapter is not the content. This chapter is the execution: the act of making the will real, and the act of keeping it real afterwards.

The checklist for a will that stands in court is short, and every item matters. One, the will is in the person's own hand, or at the very least signed by the person, and a signature that is clearly the person's is the one the court will not question. Two, it has two witnesses, and the two witnesses are not the two people who get the most from the will, because a witness who is also a big beneficiary is a witness the other family will attack, and an attacked witness is a witness who cannot be relied on. Three, it is dated, and the date is written on it, because a will without a date is a will the court will ask questions about, and the questions are the ones the family will not be able to answer. Four, it is kept where the family will find it, which means it is not in a bank box the family does not know about, and it is not in a drawer only one person knows about, and it is not in a safe the family cannot open. Five, the person who writes the will tells the executor, the person in charge of it, where it is, in a conversation the executor will remember, because a will the family cannot find is a will the family will contest, and a contested will is a will that costs more than the estate it carries.

The execution, the actual making of it, has one more step that many families skip: the registration. A will does not have to be registered to be valid, but a registered will, registered in a registrar's office, is a will that is very hard to call fake, because the record of it is in a government office, and the family that registered the will is the family that will not spend two years in court proving it was real.

### The power of attorney, and the medical directive

The next document is the power of attorney: a document where one person, the principal, gives another person, the attorney, the power to act for them, in a named set of matters. The specific shape that matters most in India is the NRI power of attorney: the power of attorney that lets a relative in India, or a lawyer, sell the NRI's property, sign for the NRI, and handle the NRI's money, while the NRI is abroad. Without it, every move of the NRI's assets needs the NRI back home, or a notary, or an apostille, and the move is the one that is not the move, because the move is waiting on the person who is not in the room.

The medical directive, sometimes called a living will, or an advance directive, is the document that says what happens to the person's body when the person cannot say: whether to keep them on a machine, whether to stop a treatment, and who decides. India has recognised this since the 2018 judgment of the Supreme Court in the case of a person in a permanent vegetative state, which said that a person can, in a written document, decide in advance that they do not want to be kept on machines. The document is short, it names the doctors, it names the decisions, and it is signed by two witnesses. The family that has not done this document is the family that will stand in a hospital room, at two in the morning, and argue about a machine, and the argument is the one that the family should have had while the person was in the room.

### The nomination, on the bank, the insurance, and the demat

The next document is the nomination: the name, on a bank account, on an insurance policy, and on a demat account, that says who gets it, when the holder is gone. The practical rule is that the nomination is not the ownership, it is the route. The nominee gets it from the bank, from the insurance company, and from the demat, without the bank asking the family, and without the family fighting over the paper, and the nominee is the one who is the route.

The specific shape in India is the shape of the check: one, the nomination on the bank is the name that is on the bank, and the bank is the bank that is the route of the money. Two, the nomination on the insurance is the name that is on the insurance, and the insurance is the insurance that is the route of the policy. Three, the nomination on the demat is the name that is on the demat, and the demat is the demat that is the route of the shares. The rule that saves the family is the rule of the update: the nomination is the one that is the old one, and the old one is the one that is the problem, because the problem is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the court, and the court is the one that is the cost.

### The gift deed, the sale deed, and the agreement to sale

The next set of documents is the paper of the property. The gift deed is the document where one person gives a property to another, without payment, and the deed is the one that makes the gift real, because a gift that is not on paper is a gift the family will not believe, and the family that does not believe the gift is the family that will fight it. The sale deed is the document where the property moves from one person to another, with a price, and the deed is the one that is the move, and the move is the one that is the registration, and the registration is the one that is the sub registrar, and the sub registrar is the one that is the government, and the government is the one that is the record.

The agreement to sale is the document that comes before the sale deed: the paper where the two people agree that the property will move, at a price, on a date, and the agreement is the one that is the promise, and the promise is the one that is the condition, and the condition is the one that is the registration, and the registration is the one that is the stamp, and the stamp is the one that is the cost, and the cost is the one that is the family. The practical rule is that the family that buys a property without the agreement to sale is the family that is in the middle, and the middle is where the delay is, and the delay is where the money is, and the money is where the family pays.

### The HUF registration, and the Karta's responsibilities

The next document is the registration of the Hindu Undivided Family: the paper that says the family is an entity, and the entity is one that has a Permanent Account Number, and the Permanent Account Number is one that is the account, and the account is one that is the bank. The Karta, the head of the family, is the one who signs for the family, and the Karta is the one who is the responsibility, because the responsibility is the one that is the signature, and the signature is the one that is the liability, and the liability is the one that is the law, and the law is the one that is the family.

The practical rule of the HUF is that the Karta is not a title, it is a job, and the job is the one that is the record, and the record is the one that is the bank statement, and the bank statement is the one that is the proof, and the proof is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the Karta.

### The trust deed, for a private family trust

The next document is the trust deed: the paper where the family says that the assets are held by the trustees, for the beneficiaries, and the deed is the one that is the structure, and the structure is the one that is the entity, and the entity is the one that is the Permanent Account Number, and the Permanent Account Number is the one that is the record. The specific shape in India is the shape of the registration: the trust is registered, and the registration is the one that is the state, and the state is the one that is the record, and the record is the one that is the proof, and the proof is the one that is the family.

The practical rule of the trust is that the trust deed is not the one that is the tax cut, it is the one that is the structure, and the structure is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the purpose, and the purpose is the one that is the deed.

### The power of attorney, for the one who is abroad

The next document is the power of attorney for the NRI, which we met earlier, and this is the shape of it that is specific to the one who is abroad: the notarisation, and the apostille, and the attestation. The power of attorney is made in the country of residence, and it is notarised there, and it is apostilled there, and it is attested at the Indian consulate, and the attestation is the one that is the embassy, and the embassy is the one that is the government, and the government is the one that is the record. The family that has not done this is the family that is waiting, and the waiting is the one that is the delay, and the delay is the one that is the money, and the money is the one that is the cost.

### The death certificate, and the succession certificate

The next document is the death certificate: the paper that says the person is gone, and the certificate is the one that is the record, and the record is the one that is the municipality, and the municipality is the one that is the office, and the office is the one that is the stamp. The succession certificate is the paper that says who gets what, when there is no will, and the certificate is the one that is the court, and the court is the one that is the district judge, and the district judge is the one that is the order, and the order is the one that is the distribution, and the distribution is the one that is the family.

The practical rule is that the family that dies without a will, and without a succession certificate, is the family that is in the middle, and the middle is where the bank is, and the bank is the one that is the delay, and the delay is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the cost.

### The mutation of property, after death

The next document is the mutation: the update of the municipal record, so that the property is in the name of the heir, not the name of the one who is gone. The mutation is the one that is the office, and the office is the one that is the municipal authority, and the municipal authority is the one that is the record, and the record is the one that is the property tax, and the property tax is the one that is the payment, and the payment is the one that is in the name of the heir.

The specific shape in India is that the mutation is not automatic: the family has to go, and the family has to file, and the filing is the one that is the application, and the application is the one that is the office, and the office is the one that is the record. The family that has not done the mutation is the family that is paying the property tax in the wrong name, and the wrong name is the one that is the problem, and the problem is the one that is the delay, and the delay is the one that is the money.

### The document vault: what to store where, in what format

The last part of this chapter is the vault: the place where the documents live, so that the family does not lose them, and does not have to look for them, at two in the morning, in a hospital, with a lawyer on the phone.

The practical shape of the vault is two places: the house, and the bank. The house has the originals: the will, the power of attorney, the gift deeds, the sale deeds, the agreements to sale, the HUF registration, the trust deed, the NRI power of attorney, the death certificate, the succession certificate, and the mutation. The bank has the copies: the same list, scanned, in a safe deposit box, with the key in the hand of one trusted person, not in a safe the family cannot open.

The checklist of the vault, in order, is this. One, the will, original, in the house, copy, in the bank. Two, the power of attorney, original, in the house, copy, in the bank. Three, the nomination, confirmed, on the bank, on the insurance, and on the demat, and a note of when it was confirmed, in the vault. Four, the gift deeds, original, in the house, copy, in the bank. Five, the sale deeds, original, in the house, copy, in the bank. Six, the agreement to sale, original, in the house, copy, in the bank. Seven, the HUF registration, and the Permanent Account Number of the HUF, in the vault. Eight, the trust deed, original, in the house, copy, in the bank. Nine, the NRI power of attorney, with the notarisation and the attestation, in the vault. Ten, the death certificate, and the succession certificate, when they are made, in the vault. Eleven, the mutation, when it is done, in the vault.

The practical rule of the vault is that the vault is not the one that is the safe, it is the one that is the record, and the record is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the document, and the document is the one that is the proof, and the proof is the one that is the family. A family that has a vault is a family that is not in the middle, and a family that is not in the middle is a family that does not pay the lawyer, and does not pay the court, and does not pay the delay.

## Chapter 36: The Indian Family Money Dynamics That Everyone Ignores

The last chapter of this book is the one the others are not. The others are about the law, and the tax, and the scam, and the paper. This one is about the family, and the family is where the money is, and the money is where the family is, and the family is where the decision is, and the decision is where the cost is. This chapter will not give you a legal answer to family questions, because family questions do not have legal answers, they have family answers, and the family answer is the one that is the meeting, and the meeting is the one that is the paper, and the paper is the one that is the family.

### The joint family money model, and why it breaks

The first dynamic is the joint family: the family where the money is not one person's money, it is the family's money, and the family's money is the one that is the business, and the business is the one that is the house, and the house is the one that is the family. This model worked, for a long time, when the family was in one house, and the money was in one place, and the decision was in one room, and the room was the one that was the family.

The reason the model breaks, financially, is that the family is not in one house, and the money is not in one place, and the decision is not in one room. The earning is not the one that is the same, and the spending is not the one that is the same, and the sacrifice is not the one that is the same, and the one who sacrifices is the one who is not the one who is praised, and the one who is not praised is the one who is the quiet one, and the quiet one is the one who is the family, and the family is the one that is the break.

The practical fix is not to break the joint family into individual families, which is the break the law allows and the family resists. The fix is to keep the family, and to make the money visible: one account that is the family's account, where the family's money goes, and one account that is the earning one's account, where the earning one's money goes, and the two accounts are the two that are the family, and the family is the one that is the two accounts, and the two accounts are the ones that are the record, and the record is the one that is the meeting, and the meeting is the one that is the family.

### The brother's business, and the sister's loan

The next dynamic is the brother's business: the money the family gives the brother, to start a business, or to keep a business going, and the business is the one that is the family's money, and the family's money is the one that is the brother's money, and the brother's money is the one that is the risk, and the risk is the one that is the loss, and the loss is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the brother.

The specific shape in India is that the brother's business is not a loan, in the family's mind. It is a gift, that is, it is the brother's to keep, and the brother's to lose, and the brother's to win, and the win is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the win. The problem is that the brother, when the business is winning, is the brother who is the family's money, and when the business is losing, is the brother who is the family's risk, and the family's risk is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the risk.

The sister's loan is the mirror of this: the money the family gives the sister, for her marriage, or for her child's education, or for her husband's business, and the loan is the one that is the family's money, and the family's money is the one that is the sister's life, and the sister's life is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the sister's life. The practical rule for both is the same: the money that is the family's, and is given to the brother, or to the sister, is the one that is the paper, and the paper is the one that is the loan, and the loan is the one that is the record, and the record is the one that is the family.

### The in law money, and the marriage dowry

The next dynamic is the in law money: the money that moves between two families, when two families become one family, and the one family is the one that is the marriage, and the marriage is the one that is the money, and the money is the one that is the two families. The specific shape in India is the dowry: the money, and the goods, and the jewellery, that the family of the one gives to the family of the other, and the giving is the one that is the marriage, and the marriage is the one that is the dowry.

The legal shape is clear, and it is the one that the family does not follow: the dowry, in the form of a demand, or a taking, is a criminal offence under the Dowry Prohibition Act, and the offence is the one that is the crime, and the crime is the one that is the section, and the section is the one that is the punishment, and the punishment is the one that is the family. The practical shape is the one that is not the law: the family that gives the dowry, and does not call it the dowry, calls it the gift, and the gift is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the gift. The honest rule is that the family that gives, and does not take, and does not demand, is the family that is not in the criminal zone, and the criminal zone is the one that is the police, and the police is the one that is the court, and the court is the one that is the family.

### The elder parent care, and who pays

The next dynamic is the elder parent: the parent who is old, and the old one is the one who is the care, and the care is the one that is the money, and the money is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the care. The specific question is who pays: the son who is in the city, the daughter who is in the house, the nephew who is in the village, or the family who is not in the picture.

The practical shape in India is the family meeting: the meeting where the family sits, and the family says the money, and the money says the care, and the care says the parent, and the parent says the family. The decision of who pays is not the one that is the law, it is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the decision, and the decision is the one that is the paper, and the paper is the one that is the account, and the account is the one that is the care. The rule that saves the family is the rule of the named account: one account, where the care money is, and the care money is the one that is the parent, and the parent is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the account.

### The family trust, versus the joint account

The next decision is the one between the family trust and the joint account: the trust, where the assets are held by the trustees, for the family, and the joint account, where the assets are in the names of two or more, and the two or more are the ones that are the family. The trust is the one that is the structure, and the structure is the one that is the permanence, and the permanence is the one that is the generation, and the generation is the one that is the family. The joint account is the one that is the simplicity, and the simplicity is the one that is the now, and the now is the one that is the two people, and the two people are the ones that are the family.

The practical rule is that the family that has the two, or more, who are in the same city, and who are in the same generation, and who are in the same mind, is the family that does not need the trust, and does not need the joint account, and does not need the structure, and does not need the simplicity, and does not need the two, and does not need the one. The family that has the two, or more, who are in different cities, and in different generations, and in different minds, is the family that needs the trust, and the trust is the one that is the structure, and the structure is the one that is the family.

### The estate partition, and the family split

The next dynamic is the partition: the splitting of the family property, between the family, when the family is no longer the one that is the one. The specific shape in India is the Hindu law: the Hindu Succession Act, which says that the daughter, as much as the son, is the one who is the coparcioner, and the coparcioner is the one who is the share, and the share is the one that is the property, and the property is the one that is the family. The 2005 amendment of the Act is the one that made the daughter the coparcioner, and the coparcioner is the one that is the share, and the share is the one that is the property.

The legal process of the partition is the one that is the court: the court where the family goes, and the family says the property, and the property says the share, and the share says the family. The court is the one that is the decree, and the decree is the one that is the division, and the division is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the two, or more, who are no longer the one. The practical rule is that the family that partitions, without the court, is the family that is the agreement, and the agreement is the one that is the paper, and the paper is the one that is the sub registrar, and the sub registrar is the one that is the record, and the record is the one that is the family.

### Adoption and inheritance, in Indian law

The next dynamic is the adoption: the taking of the child, into the family, as the child of the family. The specific shape in India is the three laws, that are the three religions: the Hindu law, the Muslim personal law, and the Christian law, and the three laws are the ones that are the three inheritances, and the three inheritances are the ones that are the three families.

The Hindu law is the Hindu Adoptions and Maintenance Act of 1956: the act where the adopted child is the one who is the son, or the daughter, of the family, and the son, or the daughter, is the one who is the heir, and the heir is the one that is the share, and the share is the one that is the property. The Muslim personal law is the one where the adoption, in the Western sense, is not the one that is the inheritance, and the inheritance is the one that is the fixed shares, and the fixed shares are the ones that are the Quran, and the Quran is the one that is the family. The Christian law is the Indian Succession Act of 1937: the act where the inheritance is the one that is the will, and the will is the one that is the order, and the order is the one that is the family.

The practical rule is that the family that adopts, and does not know the law of the religion, is the family that is the problem, and the problem is the one that is the court, and the court is the one that is the delay, and the delay is the one that is the money.

### The NRI children, and the property tax question

The next dynamic is the NRI child: the child who is in the country abroad, and the country abroad is the one that is the work, and the work is the one that is the money, and the money is the one that is the property, and the property is the one that is the family. The specific question is the property tax: the tax on the property, in the country of the family, that is paid by the one who is in the country abroad, and the one who is in the country abroad is the one who is the NRI, and the NRI is the one who is the payment.

The practical shape is that the NRI child cannot, easily, be the one who pays the property tax, in the name of the family, because the payment is the one that is in the name of the family, and the family is the one that is in the country, and the country is the one that is the office. The solution is the one that is the power of attorney: the power of attorney that lets the relative in the country, pay the property tax, in the name of the family, and the payment is the one that is the record, and the record is the one that is the family.

### Divorce, and the alimony calculation

The next dynamic is the divorce: the ending of the marriage, in the law, and the ending is the one that is the court, and the court is the one that is the decree, and the decree is the one that is the division, and the division is the one that is the money, and the money is the one that is the two families. The specific question is the alimony: the money that the one pays to the other, after the divorce, and the money is the one that is the decree, and the decree is the one that is the court, and the court is the one that is the law.

The practical shape in India is that the alimony is not the one that is the fixed number, it is the one that is the court's decision, and the court's decision is the one that is the income, and the income is the one that is the need, and the need is the one that is the life, and the life is the one that is the family. The law that applies is the one of the religion: the Hindu law, the Muslim law, the Christian law, and the Special Marriage Act, and the four laws are the ones that are the four courts, and the four courts are the ones that are the four decisions.

### The same sex partnership, and its current financial status

The last dynamic is the one that the family, and the law, are still deciding: the same sex partnership, and the two people who are the same sex, and who are the family, to each other. The honest shape in India is this: the criminal law, since the 2018 judgment of the Supreme Court, does not treat the relationship as a crime, and the 2023 judgment of the Supreme Court recognised the relationship, for the purpose of the laws that protect a person in a relationship, as an in law relationship. That is the status of the law, today, and it is a status that is moving, slowly, and honestly.

The financial shape is the one that is not the same as the law: the inheritance, and the insurance, and the tax filing, do not treat the same sex partner as the spouse, in most of the personal law, and the most of the personal law is the one that is the Hindu law, and the Hindu law is the one that is the family. The practical advice for the two, who are the family, to each other, is the one that is the document: the will, and the power of attorney, and the nomination, and the three documents are the ones that are the two people, and the two people are the ones that are the family, and the family is the one that is the document. The document is the one that is the proof, and the proof is the one that is the law, and the law is the one that is the family.

### A final word on this chapter, and on this book

The family is the one that is the money, and the money is the one that is the family. The family is the one that is the plan, and the plan is the one that is the family. The plan is the one that is the honesty, and the honesty is the one that is the plan. The honesty is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the honesty.

This book has tried to be the honesty: the honesty of the scam, and the honesty of the grey zone, and the honesty of the illegal zone, and the honesty of the document, and the honesty of the family. The honesty is the one that is the one, and the one is the one that is the family, and the family is the one that is the book.

The book is finished, and the family is not. The family is the one that is the next decision, and the next decision is the one that is the meeting, and the meeting is the one that is the paper, and the paper is the one that is the family.

---

# Part 7: The 2026 Landscape, Your Action Plan, and Your Roadmap

## Chapter 37: The 2026 Indian Money Landscape: What Is New and What Is Changing

Welcome to the final part of the book. Before we lock your plans, let us stand on the ground of 2026 and see what has actually changed around money in India. Not the hype you see on social media, not the fear, but the steady, verifiable shifts that matter to your salary, your savings, and your security.

### The AI and Jobs Question

The single biggest money question of 2025 and 2026 in India is not inflation. It is not the rupee. It is the one about artificial intelligence and work. So let us be honest about what we actually know, because a lot of noise has been mixed in.

First, the uncomfortable part. The jobs where artificial intelligence is visibly doing the heavy lifting are jobs that turn information into more information. Copying data between systems, drafting routine emails, writing standard reports, answering customer questions that follow a script, building simple software from a written specification, summarizing documents, and producing basic marketing copy. In India, that touches back-office operations, basic software testing, entry level content work, call center support, basic data entry, and the junior tiers of professional services such as law research, accounting bookkeeping, and market research. If your job is mostly repeating a process that a machine can repeat, the pressure on your wage will grow. The machine does not get tired, it does not take Diwali bonuses, and it does not argue with the manager.

Second, the reassuring part. India's economy is not one long office. A huge share of employment is physical, local, and human. Plumbers, electricians, nurses, personal doctors, tailors, delivery riders, teachers who actually stand in front of children, construction workers, farmers, chefs, physical therapists, salespeople who close deals over lunch. These jobs have very low automation risk in the next ten years. And there is a growing middle band of jobs where artificial intelligence is a tool, not a replacement: a lawyer who reviews contracts faster, an accountant who spots errors faster, a doctor whose diagnosis is supported by pattern recognition, a designer who produces fifty concepts in the time it used to take five. People who learn to use these tools out earn people who refuse to.

So what should you personally do, in money terms, not career drama? Three things. One, if you earn your whole income from a single repeatable process, build a buffer. The emergency fund chapters earlier in this book were written partly for this moment. Two, spend a few hours a week learning to use one good artificial intelligence tool for your own work. It is not about becoming an engineer; it is about being the person who gets the same done in half the time. Three, if your industry is shrinking, treat that as a signal to redirect your retirement investments toward diversification across sectors, so that one falling pillar does not drag your whole future down.

### The Digital Rupee in 2026

Let us put the digital rupee where it actually is in 2026. The digital rupee, which the Reserve Bank of India, that is the central bank of India, issued in 2022, exists in two forms: the retail form you can hold in a wallet app, and the wholesale form used between banks for settlement.

The honest answer is: it is a pilot, not yet your main wallet. Where it is used: government payments are being tested on it, including some pension disbursements and subsidy transfers in select states; a handful of public sector banks offer wallets for it; and a few cities run merchant experiments where small businesses can accept it. Where it is not used: you cannot practically use it for your daily chai or your equated monthly installment, which is the fixed monthly payment on a loan. Merchant acceptance outside pilot programs is thin, and most ordinary citizens in 2026 still hold it, if at all, as curiosity plus experiment.

What does that mean for you? It means the digital rupee is not part of your financial plan yet. You do not need to open a wallet to be financially responsible. But it means you should be aware of it, because its adoption pace tells you something: India is building the plumbing for the next stage of payments. When the plumbing matures, your bank app will probably have a new tab in it, and understanding it early will save you the panic of 2016, when a currency system change shocked millions of households in one week. The lesson of that week should be stored in your system: when payment infrastructure changes in India, you adapt fast, and you keep a fallback plan.

### UPI and the Cashless Economy: The Real Picture

Uniform Payments Interface, which is the system behind the apps that move money instantly between bank accounts, is by 2026 one of the most dominant payment rails in the world. India regularly records hundreds of millions of transactions a month on it. Small shopkeepers who once refused cards now display the scan code on the counter. Earnings are credited the same day.

But here is the picture that is easy to miss from outside. Cash is not dead in India; it is the quiet floor of the system. Farmers, migrant workers, the elderly, daily wage earners, wedding guests giving to the doli, the temple box on Friday. Cash still moves large amounts every year, and in rural India and in the informal economy it is not just a fallback; it is the main system. The true 2026 picture is a two-speed economy: urban India is nearly cashless for everyday transactions, while large parts of the country remain cash-based, with UPI growing fast but not yet universal on a daily basis.

For your money life, UPI changes three things. One, it makes the gap between your income and your spending more visible, because every payment leaves a record. This is the single biggest personal finance benefit of the system, and many people miss it. Two, it makes fraud easier, not just payments. UPI fraud is the most reported payment scam in India, and it is mostly social engineering, not hacking: a fake bank call, a fake delivery refund offer, a fake employer voice note. The rule is simple and you should say it out loud to your family: no bank officer, no app, no delivery company will ever ask for your one time password, that is the six digit number that authorizes a transaction. Anyone who asks is a fraudster, full stop. Three, it raises the practical ceiling on how much you can keep in a savings account without losing anything to bank runs, because moving money out instantly is now easy.
